@@137165712 <h> How Ted Turner sold newspapers in Nigeria <p> Global TV avatar , Ted Turner , whose audacious reasoning and business brinksmanship gave the world CNN , a pioneering effort in 24-hour broadcasting , passed last week at the age of 87 . The global community has been talking about the man who displayed esoteric bravery and bravado to point the way forward and has left us with so much to talk about achievements that can be cramped into a single lifetime . <p> In parabolic terms , he was a media elephant who could hardly be described from a narrow perspective . But at another level , he maintained the speed of a gazelle and soared like the eagle ; he soared so high that he homogenised the world with one smart move . CNN . <p> CNN is the Cable News Network . Whe it started out in the 80s , they pooh-poohed the station as the Chicken Noodle Network , a befitting parody of a broadcast business that was expected to fail in no time . <p> Robert Edward Turner 111 did not fail . He was @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ risks mocked failure and even death in the face . The world expected a fall at any time that would eclipse a loud-mouthed fellow . That fall did n't happen . <p> Advertisement <p> When he died on May 6 , 2026 , the world knew it had lost one of the very best , a comet whose light would blaze into eternity . First Lady of global television , Christiane Amanpour , herself one of the biggest living creations of CNN , called him Ted Turner , the Globalist . This , for me , remains a beautiful but simple appreciative epitomisation of the world that Ted Turner influenced with a strong image from a very troubled background , telling the story of the world from America 's point of view . <p> From this part of the world , we were only more acquainted with his globalism and little about his background which , for me , looks quite significant because of the way it would shape his entire life . <p> His father , Robert Turner Jr. , ran the Turner Outdoor Advertising company in Georgia where @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Brown University before he was dispatched from the school following a history of bad behaviour , including bigotry , excessive drinking and womanising . <p> Advertisement <p> Ted had all the vices in his pouch but he learnt from his father whose life was tempestuous ; an alcoholic and a serial drug abuser . Having fallen into debt , he got depressed and committed suicide in 1963 . <p> The dark side of the real world opened up to 24-year old Ted Turner who felt lost and insecure . He too became suicidal and never really thought that he could live to the age of his father before he would commit suicide . But fate works in different ways . In spite of living life on the edge and courting death and disaster always , providence was kind to him , decorating him with good credentials . <p> Against advice from his father 's friends , he refused to sell his father 's outdoor advertising business but ran it with youthful strength , energy and creativity . But the results were not the best . In 1970 , he bought @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . It was the beginning of a life that would change the history of television broadcasting globally . <p> He was heavily into sports -- Yachting , bought the Atlanta Braves in 1976 , Atlanta Hawks in 1977 , and much later , in 1984 , promoted the Goodwill Games in response to the Olympic boycotts of 1980 and 1984 , and to heal the frosty relationship between the US and Soviet Union , following the Cold War era . <p> Advertisement <p> He was truly a globalist but it is the broadcast part of his life that fascinates us here . In Ted Turner , you see the rambunctious exemplification of Marshal McLuhan 's concept of the global village , a time when electronic media technology would destroy barriers and boundaries and reduce the world into a small whole with instant communications . McLuhan 's prediction was in the 60s ; in the 80s , Turner made it an intrusive reality . <p> At the beginning , CNN haemorrhaged red ink , losing $2m monthly . Turner stayed on . You know what they say , about the patient @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ patient anyway , he was totally restless in his ways but what seems to be his madness and chaotic living had a pattern . In all this , he had his eyes fixed on the world and Nigeria became a beneficiary . <p> In 1990 , the Gulf War broke out starting with the attack on Kuwait by the Iraqi leader Saddam Hussain . A coalition led by the US rallied together to defend the small country and protect other neighbours like Saudi Arabia that would be immediate target . I was fortunate to be working in one of the prestigious newspaper houses in Nigeria and even more fortunate to have influenced the installation of a satellite dish to receive direct satellite TV signals . It was a rare moment for the voice of a rookie reporter to be heard in such a significant decision , and it was a win-win for everybody . <p> In those days , Media houses depended a lot on NAN , AFP , Reuters and the Network News of NTA at 9pm . The coming of CNN overwhelmed these other sources nearly into insignificance @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , the reporter could almost fancy himself/herself at the war front , almost a Peter Arnett reporting from Baghdad , except that the CNN reporter was confronting a terrible situation instead of the realtime live movies we were watching in the newsroom . <p> Advertisement <p> At the time , the newspapers depended on copy sales and advertising to run operations and pay salaries . During the Gulf War , copy sales tripled . Instead of two editions of the Newspapers -- Northern and Two Lagos West editions , with one of the editions featuring the very latest news on the War . People were only too happy to buy copies as satellite dishes were a preserve of a tiny class ; erroneously canvassed and accepted that only the rich could afford them ! <p> Ted Turner may have been making losses with CNN in far away United States but for some newspapers in Nigeria , it was harvest time , a boom season occasioned by one man 's vision buoyed by ever unfolding technology . <p> Advertisement <p> Since then so much have happened . Global TV became a @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and hotel rooms . Among top hotel brands that I have been privileged to patronise in different parts of the world , whether in Europe , Middle East , Asia , North America or even South America , CNN is a constant factor , a primary demand by customers . <p> CNN was at Tiananmen Square in June 1989 to broadcast live the taking down of students who had led a seven-week bruising pro-democracy protests , demanding for reforms from the Chinese authorities . The government sent in soldiers to clear the Square not minding the bones that would be broken or the deaths recorded . It was typical China where trauma could be introduced to protect the state . <p> Advertisement <p> In the final hours of Mikhail Gorbachev in office in December 1991 signalling the death of the Soviet Union , CNN was present to document it for its global audience and for posterity . When Gorbachev 's pen failed to write and he stretched his hand for help , it was the network 's President Tom Johnson who handed his pen to him to sign away the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ they were . CNN had earned a name ; respected by governments , authorities and even eccentric statesmen , among others . <p> The boom came for Turner who was now respected and courted at home and abroad . He married Holly Wood siren , Jane Fonda , whose fame reduced him to a number two in their social ranking . The marriage lasted 10 years . She was the third in the series . <p> Advertisement <p> CNN went into merger and acquisition until it was finally acquired . Billions rolled in for Turner who put his money to good use . He became a philanthropist , a Climate Change and Nuclear Weapons advocate . In 1997 , he donated a whopping $1bn to the United Nations at $100m annually . He wanted to do something that could alleviate the plight of the weak , the helpless and the endangered around the world . <p> Turner set out as a man desperate to live life at the fullest . He achieved that and left so much more for the world to be grateful for . His global television leaves @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ peace of the world in our small corners or in high political offices . But I will always remember the business boom he brought to our newspapers in the 90s . <p> May his soul find a perfect place in eternity even as his memories remain perpetually assured in this part of the divide . <p> Views expressed by contributors are strictly personal and not of TheCable.

City . <p> Despite its modest land size , the republic has built a stable economy supported by tourism , finance and manufacturing activities . <p> The country equally maintains one of Europe 's lowest unemployment rates and consistently records high living standards among its citizens . <p> One of the most notable moments in San Marino 's diplomatic history came in 1861 when former US international visitors each year . <p> The country has also become central to global discussions surrounding climate change because of fears linked to rising sea levels .

This is not only Hong Kong 's first scientific payload deployed on the national space station , but also a historic breakthrough for the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ project fully demonstrates Hong Kong 's strong capability to build national-level cutting-edge scientific payloads , to participate in long-term space station missions , and to play a key role in addressing global climate change while serving the nation 's strategic " carbon peak and carbon neutrality " goals . <p> The project is led by an interdisciplinary research team from HKUST , comprising experts from the Department of Civil and Environmental Engineering , the Division of Emerging Interdisciplinary Areas , the Division of Environment and Sustainability , the Department of Computer Science and Engineering , and the Division of Public Policy . In late 2024 , the project received formal approval from the Technology and Engineering Center for Space Utilization of the Chinese Academy of Sciences ( CSU.CAS ) , the general research center for the Space Utilization System . It is jointly developed with the CAS Changchun Institute of Optics , Fine Mechanics and Physics , and funded by the Special Call ( Aerospace Technology ) of the Innovation and Technology Support Program under the Innovation and Technology Commission of the HKSAR Government . <p> MUSICO is a lightweight @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of accurately measuring CO2 and CH4 -- two major greenhouse gases -- from space . Smaller than a domestic washing machine , the instrument maintains extra-high spectral resolution and one hundred-meter spatial resolution . By analyzing intensity changes of sunlight over specific spectral bands as it passes through the atmosphere and reflects off the Earth 's surface , MUSICO identifies gas-specific absorption features to determine gas concentrations and pinpoint individual emission sources , enabling effective monitoring of key facilities such as power plants and landfills . <p> Prof. SUN Dong , Secretary for Innovation , Technology and Industry of the HKSAR Government , remarked , " This HKUST led project represents Hong Kong 's first scientific payload aboard the Tiangong Space Station , a major milestone for Hong Kong in the nation 's space missions . The national 15th Five Year Plan identifies accelerating green and low carbon transition , building a space powerhouse , and achieving carbon peak as key priorities . The successful deployment of a payload autonomously developed by a Hong Kong research team on Tiangong powerfully demonstrates that Hong Kong scientists possess top tier research and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and green low carbon science , providing the nation with high quality , verifiable scientific data to accelerate the realization of the dual carbon goals . " <p> Prof. Nancy IP , President of HKUST , said , " We are deeply honored that our HKUST research team can participate in scientific missions on the national space station , and we sincerely thank the nation and the HKSAR Government for their long-standing trust and support . This project not only highlights HKUST 's accumulated strengths in aerospace engineering , satellite remote sensing , and environmental engineering , but also proves that Hong Kong 's research capabilities can make substantial contributions on the nation 's highest-level aerospace platforms . As the world 's first lightweight high-precision greenhouse gas point-source detection payload , MUSICO will continue to operate on the national space station , providing autonomous , controllable , high-reliability CO2 and CH4 emission monitoring data that directly support the nation 's dual-carbon goals and provide scientific backing for the ' Beautiful China ' initiative and global climate governance . " <p> President Ip added , " In recent years , HKUST @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ satellite technology -- following the successful launch of Hong Kong 's first higher-education satellite in 2023 , we are now actively participating in the nation 's Chang'e-8 lunar exploration mission . Looking ahead , HKUST will continue to leverage its strengths in artificial intelligence , robotics , and materials science to accelerate the translation of space-related technologies and nurture high-end talent , contributing to the nation 's accelerated drive to build a space powerhouse and achieve green , low-carbon , high-quality development . " <p> Prof. SU Hui , Project Lead , Chair Professor of the Department of Civil and Environmental Engineering and Global STEM Professor at HKUST , said , " The development of MUSICO involved overcoming multiple critical technical challenges -- namely , how to achieve high spectral resolution , fine spatial resolution , and synergistic multi-gas observation under strict size and weight constraints . The team conducted extensive testing and optimization in optical design , precision manufacturing , and system integration to ensure the instrument can deliver reliable , accurate greenhouse gas data over long periods under high-speed operation and extreme space environments . Successfully integrating these @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and scientific achievement , demonstrating that the technology has reached internationally advanced standards . " <p> Prof. ZHANG Limin , Co-Project Lead , National Engineer Awardee and Head of the Department of Civil and Environmental Engineering at HKUST , emphasized , " MUSICO 's observational coverage spans most land and ocean areas across low-to-mid-latitude regions , providing consistent and comparable greenhouse gas monitoring data for different regions . The project 's results will be shared with government agencies and research institutions , supporting scientific research and practical applications in the Guangdong-Hong Kong-Macao Greater Bay Area and in other parts of China . They will also provide a scientific basis for cross-regional climate research and emission reduction efforts along the Belt and Road , contributing to the global response to climate change . This is both a vivid example of HKUST 's research strength serving national strategy and a concrete practice of Hong Kong 's research capabilities contributing to global climate governance . " <p> The research project is co-led by Prof. SU Hui , Chair Professor of the Department of Civil and Environmental Engineering and Global STEM Professor at HKUST @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of the same department . protocols for delaying or postponing matches in extreme heat , improved cooling facilities for players and regular updates to FIFA 's heat guidelines in line with evolving scientific evidence . <p> match and climate-controlled benches for substitutes and technical staff at outdoor venues.FIFA also uses the Wet Bulb Globe Temperature ( WBGT ) system , regarded as the gold-standard method for Through a combination of practical wisdom , institutional experience , reflective analysis , and contextual understanding , Udo Jude Ilo provides a compelling roadmap for building resilient , accountable , and sustainable civil society institutions in Africa . <p> It is within this broader context that Building on Solid Ground : Primer on Resilience and Sustainability of CSOs in Africa by Udo Jude Ilo emerges as a timely and significant intervention . The book is a practical , reflective , and analytically rich contribution to the discourse on the resilience of civil society and institutional sustainability in Africa . This is why the subject matter of this book is for me both urgent and foundational . I have repeatedly argued that Africa 's development crisis is fundamentally linked to crises of leadership , poor strategies , weak institutions , and failure of governance systems . This aligns closely with the central thesis of Udo Jude Ilo 's book , namely that CSOs must move from personality-driven activism to systems-driven institutional sustainability . <p> Background to the Subject of the Book <p> The issue of sustainability of CSOs in Africa has become increasingly critical in the last three decades . Historically , many African CSOs emerged in response to governance failures , military authoritarianism , economic crises , human rights abuses , social injustice , and developmental deficits . Particularly in Nigeria , civil society played a central role in resisting military dictatorship , promoting democratic transition , advocating constitutional and electoral reforms , defending human rights , and deepening citizen participation . In addition , there are many issues which are necessary for peace @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ CSOs in Nigeria , to the exclusion of government and the private sector , such as gender equality , rights of persons with disability , environmental conservation , respect for human rights and dignity of the human person , harmful traditional practices , gender based violence , etc . <p> However , despite their historic contributions , many African CSOs remain structurally weak . A significant number depend excessively on foreign donor funding , operate with fragile governance systems , lack succession plans , and are often centred around founders rather than institutions . Udo Jude Ilo recognises these challenges clearly when he notes that many organisations struggle once donor funding ends and that sustainability must go beyond financial resources to include strategic planning , governance integrity , organisational health , networks , and operational credibility . <p> The broader African context further reinforces the relevance of this subject . Across many countries , the civic space is shrinking due to restrictive laws , harassment of activists , state surveillance , and political intolerance . Simultaneously , international donor priorities are shifting , creating severe financial uncertainty for CSOs . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ institutions . <p> It is therefore significant that Udo Jude Ilo does not treat sustainability merely as a financial issue . Rather , he frames it as an institutional challenge involving governance systems , strategic vision , leadership culture , accountability mechanisms , innovation , talent development , and community legitimacy . This multidimensional understanding of sustainability represents one of the greatest strengths of the book . <p> Overview and Structure of the Book <p> The book is organised into five thematic sections dealing with strategy development , institutional governance , talent management , fundraising , and visibility/networking . Through these themes , the author presents a compelling roadmap for building enduring institutions capable of surviving beyond donor cycles , charismatic founders , and changing political contexts . <p> Designing and Following Your North Star : Strategy Development and Implementation <p> A Rule-Based Order : Institutional Governance <p> Tending the Plants : Talent Management and Leadership Development <p> Hatching Your Chicks : Fundraising and Resource Mobilisation <p> Standing Out in the Pack : Visibility and Networking <p> This sequencing is deliberate and analytically significant . Unlike many academic texts that remain highly theoretical , the book combines conceptual clarity with practical insights drawn from lived experience within the African civic space . The author 's experience in organisations such as the Open Society Foundations ( OSF ) , Forum of Federations , and other civic institutions gives the work unusual depth and authenticity . <p> The book opens with a powerful reflection on why organisations should exist in the first place . Ilo strongly criticises the tendency of some individuals to establish NGOs primarily for financial gain , rather than service to humanity . He insists that the legitimate basis for founding a CSO must be commitment to a cause bigger than oneself . This moral framing is extremely important in the Nigerian context where the proliferation of " briefcase NGOs " , " political NGOs " and the phenomenon of " NGIs " have undermined public trust in civil society . <p> One of the strongest @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ CSOs in Africa 's development and democratic governance . the source of the emission , the extent of exposure risk , and possible associated gases such as hydrogen sulphide ( H2S ) and volatile organic compounds ( VOCs ) . <p> WHAT IS METHANE -- THE GAS AFFECTING THE OGUN COMMUNITY ? <p> Methane ( CH4 ) is a short-lived , colourless and highly flammable greenhouse gas that traps more than 80 @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ period . <p> Advertisement <p> It is produced when organic matter breaks down in the absence of sufficient oxygen . Its sources include oil and gas activities , dump sites , livestock , swamps , wetlands , coal mining , sewage and dirty drains , as well as rice farming . <p> About 40 percent of global human-caused methane emissionscome from agriculture , largely from livestock farming and rice cultivation . Fossil fuels contribute about 35 percent , with oil and gas accounting for about 20 -- 25 percent and coal mining contributing about 10 -- 15 percent , while waste from landfills and wastewater make up around 5 percent . <p> High concentrations of methane in the air can displace oxygen , making it difficult for people to breathe properly . Exposure may cause symptoms such as headaches , dizziness , fatigue , difficulty breathing , and fainting . In extreme cases , it can lead to suffocation and death due to oxygen deprivation . <p> Also , methane in high concentrations , when exposed to fire or sparks , can cause a severe explosion . <p> Advertisement <p> @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ a major driver of climate change . It contributes to extreme heat , drought , and flooding as it traps heat in the atmosphere . <p> Emmanuel Agbo , an atmospheric and climate scientist , told TheCable that the 13,500 ppm methane concentration detected within the area is " highly abnormal " and suggests an underlying environmental issue yet to be fully disclosed . <p> Advertisement <p> " If the information is right , 13,500 parts per million does n't seem natural to me . It seems like something that was not reported in the news is going on underneath , honestly , " he said . <p> Agbo said the elevated methane concentration could indicate the presence of other harmful gases , including hydrogen sulphide ( H2S ) and volatile organic compounds ( VOCs ) in the environment . <p> " If you have a methane value of 13,000 ppm , that is likely a tracer gas pointing towards other gases . Definitely , there are other gases for sure , " he said . <p> He expressed concern over the " weak and reactive " environmental monitoring systems @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ incidents occur . <p> Agbo said regulators often focus on monitoring single pollutants while ignoring broader atmospheric risks . <p> He added that the recurrence of similar incidents within the same area in recent weeks suggests lapses in environmental oversight and enforcement . <p> " To be honest , the monitoring is not entirely up to standard . It 's more reactive than proactive , " he added . <p> " In the same area , two events have occurred in the last six weeks . So , that shows that there is no consistent arbitration system to address this thing . " <p> METHANE POLLUTION AND PUBLIC HEALTH CONCERNS IN OGUN <p> According to research , methane-related air pollution can spread to homes , schools , and healthcare facilities located up to 19 kilometres from the source . <p> Tanimola Akande , a professor of public health and consultant public health physician at the University of Ilorin , told TheCable that although methane is naturally occurring , exposure to high concentrations can become dangerous and , in severe cases , fatal . <p> Akande said the incident in Ogun @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ teenagers are more vulnerable to polluted air and toxic gas exposure . <p> " Methane exposure is toxic when the exposure level is high . It is also true that exposure to other gases can pose additional health risks , " Akande said . <p> He noted that the exposure recorded in the affected schools could have resulted in more severe outcomes if the concentration had been higher or if emergency response had been delayed . <p> According to him , children and teenagers are particularly susceptible because their lungs and immune systems are still developing , making them more likely to suffer long-term health complications , including asthma and bronchitis from repeated or prolonged exposure to polluted air . <p> Akande added that toxic gas exposure may also affect neurological development in children if the pollution persists or is not properly contained . <p> " The respiratory rate of children and teenagers is higher than that of adults , and so what is absorbed per kilogram of body weight is more among children and teenagers , " he said . <p> " In addition , the lungs and immune @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ therefore likely to have more long-term complications than adults . They also are more active outdoors than adults and so have more exposure to air pollutants . " <p> He said the recurrence of gas leak incidents in the same community points to weak environmental monitoring systems and inadequate preventive measures . <p> The public health expert called for stronger air quality monitoring , improved environmental enforcement , and the development of effective public health policies to prevent future incidents . <p> He also urged the government to properly equip regulatory agencies responsible for monitoring pollution and to intensify public education on environmental and air pollution risks .

Transport fares have climbed , food prices are moving , and the cost of basic goods continues to stretch household budgets already thinned by previous economic adjustments . <p> A fragile disinflation story begins to wobble <p> ads <p> ads <p> The rise in inflation interrupts what had been a cautiously optimistic narrative in 2025 @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ linked to fuel subsidy removal , currency pressures , and broader structural reforms . <p> Economists had , at the start of 2026 , even floated the possibility of a return to single-digit inflation . That projection now looks increasingly distant . <p> Instead , analysts are pointing to a convergence of external and domestic shocks that are steadily rebuilding inflationary momentum . <p> One of the most immediate triggers , according to analysts , is the disruption in global energy markets following the Iran -- US conflict that escalated in late February . For traders and manufacturers , every additional naira in fuel cost feeds directly into final prices . <p> The result is an economy where inflation is being driven not by a single force , but by a layered combination of imported inflation , domestic cost shocks , and structural inefficiencies . <p> What rising inflation means for households <p> For ordinary Nigerians , the implications are immediate and often harsh . <p> As Samson puts it , the reversal of earlier disinflation trends signals " reduction in purchasing power , a deepening cost-of-living crisis , further impoverishment of an already strained population , and increased economic @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ also risks slowing economic growth by discouraging investment and weakening consumer demand . @@137314344 <h> Energy Transition : Insights From The NAEE/IAEE Conference <p> I attended the recently concluded 19th edition of the Nigerian Association for Energy Economics ( NAEE ) Conference , which took place from April 26 to 29 , 2026 , at the Civic Centre , Lagos . Unfortunately , I was unable to participate in the 18th edition , which took place at the PTDF Conference Auditorium , Central Business District , Abuja , where I was scheduled to present a paper titled " Seventeen International Banks ' Withdrawal from the Net-Zero Banking Alliance : Implications for Energy Transition in Africa . " At this year 's conference , themed " Evolution of Future Energy Mix in Africa : The Role of Technology , Economics , and Public Policy , " I was also billed to present during the concurrent session on April 28 . However , I only participated as an attendee and missed delivering my scheduled presentation titled " Energy Transition Experience in Africa : Cakewalk vs Gordian Knot . " <p> The Nigerian Association for Energy Economics ( NAEE ) is the Nigerian affiliate of the International @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ conference assembled a distinguished gathering of experts , policymakers , and industry leaders for rich and stimulating discussions . At the cocktail reception on the evening of April 26 , I had the pleasure of meeting some undergraduate students . I was particularly impressed by the firmness of their support for decarbonisation and energy transition . The ongoing geopolitical tensions , especially around the Strait of Hormuz , appeared to strengthen their conviction regarding the urgent need for energy transition . <p> During a civil discussion with one student , his legendary support for the complete phase-out of fossil fuels reminded me of environmental movements like the Extinction Rebellion . Interestingly , he was wearing a t-shirt , which led me to gently point out that polyester -- a synthetic fibre derived from petroleum -- probably formed part of the t-shirt material he was putting on . This observation momentarily gave him a brief pause . <p> Leveraging the opportunity , I went on to explain that more than 90 per cent of pharmaceutical feedstocks and reagents are derived from petrochemicals , not to mention the vast array of medical @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ radiological dyes , X-ray films , and personal protective equipment that keep modern healthcare functioning . However , what appeared to surprise him the most was learning that the global Leaf Area Index has been increasing approximately at 2.3 per cent per decade -- meaning the planet is greening rather than browning . This greening phenomenon is attributed predominantly to the fertilisation effect of elevated atmospheric carbon dioxide . <p> The conference officially commenced on Monday , April 27 , 2026 . Delivering his opening remarks as Chairman of the occasion was Prof. Barth Nnaji , Chairman of Geometric Power Group and a former Minister of Power . He spoke about western hypocrisy in energy policy , citing the rapid recommissioning of coal-fired power plants in response to recent geopolitical crises -- notably Iran 's disruption of the Strait of Hormuz and Russia 's invasion of Ukraine . <p> He was full of praise for the Dangote Petroleum Refinery and Petrochemicals , asserting that Nigeria would have found itself in a precarious position without the refinery . Prof. Nnaji urged governments to make clear strategic choices regarding their preferred energy @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ that the country would rely heavily on various forms of natural gas to power its economy in two decades . <p> He expressed bewilderment at Nigeria 's inability to build enough infrastructure to enable the utilisation of abundant gas resources . Connecting the inadequate feeder gas supply to the current 60 per cent operational capacity of the Nigeria Liquefied Natural Gas , Nnaji stressed the need for greater gas-sector financing and partial risk guarantees . He firmly believes Africa can become a continent of shared power -- after all , " the United States imports electricity from Canada . @@137379148 <h> Beheaded Teacher Michael was someone 's son , by Funke Egbemode <p> It is another sad day in Nigeria . Another dark day . His only sin was that he woke up and went to work , something he had been doing for years . If he had known what that fateful day had in stock for him , he would have stayed in bed . But he did not . He had no way of knowing that the monster we thought lived only in the forests of the north had finally built a hut in the south west . <p> Michael Oyedokun a schoolteacher left his house in Oyo State to go to work . He did not carry a gun . He was not a soldier marching to a battlefield . He was not a criminal fleeing justice . He was not a politician with enemies or political detractors . <p> He was a simple teacher , a man who carried books instead of bullets . His daily assignment was to help shape young minds and help children find a future brighter than the present darkness surrounding @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ to birth the next generation of engineers and pilots , doctors and nurses , tech pros and climate change experts . <p> But before the day ended , terrorists reportedly captured him and hours later , ended his life in the most gruesome manner . The darkness of Nigeria consumed him , leaving all of us bewildered . <p> Mr. Oyedokun 's only offence was going to work . That single sentence alone should break the heart of every decent human being . <p> In sane societies , teachers are protected because they build civilisation . Nations honour teachers because they prepare the next generation . Did n't we all grow up in communities that respected them because every doctor , lawyer , engineer , journalist , governor and president once sat before a teacher in a classroom ? <p> But today 's Nigeria is beheading its teachers . Today 's Nigeria is not safe , not for pupils or their teachers . How did we arrive at this cruel , sorry pass , this evil junction of blood where a man can leave his home in the morning to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ people does that make us ? <p> As far as this girl is concerned , this is no longer just insecurity . It is organised cruelty . It is the systematic destruction of human dignity . It is evil in human form . Those we call criminals are demons , monsters . Or is it not beyond frightening wickedness that there are human beings who can hold another human being down for his head to be severed as though his life meant absolutely nothing ? Does n't that level of brutality belong in nightmares , in horror films ? Why then has it become a recurring reality in Nigeria ? <p> It is easy to call out President Tinubu . It is convenient to blame this on Governor Seyi Makinde . But let us ask ourselves : Who are the people providing cover , funding and guns for these demons ? Somewhere , as you read this , someone is leaking , selling sensitive information to kidnappers . Someone is selling them daily supplies . Someone is making tons of money from this evil enterprise . They know people will @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ n't care . <p> Teacher Michael was someone 's son , a husband , a father , a breadwinner , a family 's hope until his head was taken to prove a point . <p> This is the kind of tragedy Nigeria now produces with such frightening regularity and we are gradually becoming emotionally numb , exhausted.Every week comes with fresh horror . <p> A farmer goes to his farm and never returns . <p> A trader 's blood is splattered on her wares in the market . <p> A nursing mother is abducted . <p> Students disappear on highways . <p> Youth Corps members are kidnapped in busloads . <p> Worshippers are shipped off in the middle of church service . <p> Villages are invaded in the middle of the night . <p> Children watch helplessly as their parents are slaughtered before them . <p> Entire communities sleep with fear wrapped around their throats . <p> And now , a teacher has been beheaded simply because he went to work . <p> The dead are almost never the powerful . They are ordinary Nigerians struggling honestly to survive . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ work . People who wake up every morning hoping to feed their families legitimately . People who still believe dignity can come from labour instead of crime . <p> The tragedy of this latest killing becomes even heavier when one remembers how poorly teachers are already treated in the country . Many work under terrible conditions . Poor salaries . Broken classrooms . Leaking roofs . Inadequate teaching materials . Yet they continue showing up every morning because they have to , someone has to . <p> Society hands children over to teachers daily with one request : help shape the future . <p> And this is how Nigeria rewards one of them . By allowing him to become another corpse in the growing cemetery of national sorrow . <p> Somewhere sadly as you read this , Teacher Michael 's wife is staring blankly into darkness , unable to understand how life changed so brutally , so quickly . The children are waiting for a father who will never walk through the door again . Somewhere , his relatives are crying and asking questions nobody can answer . <p> How @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ ? How does the Oyedokun family recover from this horror ? <p> Some weeks ago , I alerted us about the steady movement of these demons towards the South through Kwara State . I wrote about the less than two-hour drives and 90-minute journey between horror and once-upon-a-time safe places . The Kwara abductions frightened me sh-tless , I must confess . I told a friend the name of the town where these monsters may be headed . He laughed . Well , he is not laughing now , trust me . This thing has finally gotten out of hand . Do n't bother telling me anything different . The monster is ready to go shopping in the open . It is daring us , all of us , from President Tinubu to my youngest grandchild . We must wake up and kill it , this monster . There is no second option . <p> The fear we live with is too much , devastating , debilitating fear . Enough is enough . Fear has now become one of Nigeria 's most dominant national emotions . <p> We fear highways @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ strange motorcycles . <p> We fear unknown phone numbers . <p> We fear traveling at night . <p> We fear forests . <p> We fear sleeping deeply . <p> Parents panic until children return home safely . <p> Families pray before ordinary road journeys as though loved ones are heading into battlefields . <p> Before you put everything at the doorstep of the current administration , think deeply about how decades of decay got us here . Let 's not be shallow . Think of the years of selfish leaders , years of no planning , no focus on tangible development . Think of those who hid behind religion to impoverish those who trusted . Where we are today was long in coming but anybody with the sense God gave a goose knew we would eventually harvest what we sowed . <p> We did not start looking over our shoulders overnight . It was a gradual descent into the valley of darkness and despair . We simply left undone what we should have done . We refused to learn the simple lessons ; like terrorism does not only kill individuals @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ <p> That farmers abandoning their farms out of fear will have ripple effects . <p> Perhaps the saddest part is how Nigerians are gradually becoming used to tragedy . <p> Outrage now has a short lifespan . <p> A massacre happens . <p> People cry online . <p> Officials condemn it . <p> Burials take place . <p> Then another tragedy arrives and pushes the previous one aside . <p> The dead disappear into statistics . <p> " Ten killed . " <p> " Twenty abducted . " <p> " Thirty villagers massacred . " <p> Simple numbers . <p> But every number was a human being with dreams , plans and loved ones . Every victim had people who depended on them emotionally and financially . Some hoped to build houses , planned to marry and have three , four children . Some wanted to send their children to school abroad , give them the great starts they did n't get . Some simply wished to grow old peacefully . <p> Then evil arrived with guns , machetes and hatred and everything ended abruptly . Hopes , dreams , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> The danger of repeated violence is not only physical destruction . It is emotional numbness . A society constantly exposed to bloodshed risks losing its capacity for empathy . People become tired of mourning . Shock begins to fade . Death becomes ordinary . <p> That may be the greatest tragedy of all . <p> One can not even begin to imagine the final moments of that teacher 's life . <p> Perhaps his wife reminded him not to be late returning home . <p> Perhaps his child asked him to bring biscuits after work . <p> Perhaps he left home believing it would be another normal day . <p> Perhaps he even smiled before stepping outside . <p> Then somewhere along the journey , terror found him . <p> Students will resume lessons someday , but there will always be an invisible absence hanging in the air . @@137412364 <h> Nigeria 's climate governance strategic synergy as model for Africa <p> In a time of growing angst in our nation , one of the heartwarming developments is in the fact that other African nations increasingly look to Nigeria 's climate governance structure as a model . This is largely driven by Nigeria 's Climate Change Act of 2021 , signed by former President Muhammadu Buhari , which formally established the National Council on Climate Change to streamline fragmented climate policies into coherent national development . Unlike many African countries whose climate goals exist merely as policy documents , Nigeria 's legally binding Climate Change mandates the mainstreaming of climate actions into national budgets and economic planning . <p> Now , the President Bola Tinubu government has taken it a notch higher . In the emerging architecture of Nigeria 's climate governance , one of the most strategic developments is the convergence of three critical dimensions of climate action -- awareness , technology , and finance -- under the coordinated efforts of three presidential advisers : Yussuf Olatunji Kelani , Olamide Fagbuji , and Ibrahim Shelleng , respectively . <p> @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ distant environmental issue but a direct economic , developmental , and security challenge , the collaboration among these three presidential aides represents a deliberate attempt to build a holistic climate response system for Nigeria . Their synergy is perhaps best reflected in the conception and implementation of the Renewed Hope Climate Change Awareness Tour , a nationwide initiative designed to mainstream climate consciousness and mobilise grassroots participation across the federation . <p> The significance of this collaboration lies in the fact that climate governance can not succeed through isolated interventions . Awareness without technology remains rhetorical . Technology without financing remains theoretical . Financing without public mobilisation risks elitism and policy failure . The Tinubu administration appears to understand this interdependence , which explains why the three advisers have been strategically positioned to complement one another . <p> Kelani 's role as Special Assistant on Climate Change Matters provides the ideological and mobilisation backbone of the initiative . His public engagements consistently frame climate change as a national security and socio-economic issue rather than merely an environmental concern . In several public interventions and outreaches , Kelani has linked flooding @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and even communal instability to the climate crisis , thereby helping ordinary Nigerians understand the practical implications of environmental degradation . <p> This awareness-oriented approach is crucial in a country where climate literacy remains relatively low , particularly in rural communities . Through REHCCAT , Kelani has sought to democratise climate knowledge by taking discussions beyond policy circles and international conferences into markets , schools , local governments , communities , and state institutions . In fact , the optics of seeing him and Alhaji Aliko Dangote together in Kano when he paid an advocacy call on the renowned businessman give a boost to the promise of a new private sector-driven climate action paradigm . <p> However , awareness alone can not solve climate problems . This is where the contribution of Olamide Fagbuji becomes strategically important . As a Senior Special Assistant on Climate Technology and Operations , his portfolio introduces the innovation and operational framework necessary for practical implementation . Climate technology today sits at the centre of global green transition efforts , from renewable energy and digital environmental monitoring to smart agriculture and clean transportation systems . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ whose climate finance and stakeholder engagement portfolio injects economic realism into the climate agenda . Globally , climate action is increasingly driven by access to finance , carbon markets , green investments , and public-private partnerships . Nigeria 's climate aspirations can only materialise if supported by strong financing mechanisms capable of attracting investment and supporting implementation at the federal , state , and local levels . <p> Indeed , one of the most notable achievements of the Renewed Hope Climate Change Awareness Tour is the way it merges these three pillars into a single national framework . <p> The initiative is not designed merely as an advocacy campaign . It simultaneously seeks to educate citizens , identify bankable climate projects , train climate champions , mobilise partnerships , and create green economic opportunities . <p> The tour aims to cover multiple states across Nigeria 's six geopolitical zones while reaching millions through media and community engagement . It also intends to train climate champions in local government areas and stimulate green jobs and climate innovation ecosystems . These objectives reflect a coordinated vision that combines Kelani 's grassroots mobilisation @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ 's financing and stakeholder deployment expertise . <p> Strategically , this synergy also reflects a broader shift in Nigeria 's climate diplomacy . Climate action is increasingly being integrated into national development planning , economic diversification , youth empowerment , and international investment positioning . For instance , just a couple of days ago , the Ministry of Youth Development and its partner , People Planet and Peace Foundation , launched what they tagged ' Climate Beyond Borders Caravan ' , an Africa-wide project that promises to place young Nigerians ahead of their continental peers in the climate action ecosystem . <p> Certainly , with concerted efforts by critical stakeholders , Nigeria could be positioned not merely as a victim of climate change but as an active participant in the global green transition . The importance of this can not be overstated . As global capital increasingly flows toward sustainable economies , countries that fail to align governance , innovation , and climate finance risk economic marginalisation . <p> Therefore , I am of the view that this paradigm should be replicated at the subnational level , the same way @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , with some creating their own State Council on Climate Change . Nigeria needs a climate governance structure that combines political communication , technological innovation , and investment mobilisation . The collaboration among Kelani , Fagbuji , and Shelleng offers an early template for such integrated governance . <p> Furthermore , their work demonstrates that climate action in Nigeria must move beyond elite environmental discourse into a practical national development strategy . Flood victims in coastal communities , farmers battling desertification in the North , unemployed youths seeking green opportunities , and urban residents facing heat stress and pollution are all directly affected by climate realities . Addressing these challenges requires coordinated leadership capable of connecting policy to people . By combining public sensitisation , technological innovation , and climate financing within a unified framework , a multidimensional climate governance model could help us defeat multidimensional poverty and redefine Nigeria 's environmental future . <p> Being the most populated country in the region , Nigeria 's success will signal an important lesson for Africa : climate action succeeds when awareness inspires innovation , innovation attracts investment , and investment empowers @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ interagency collaboration and multi-stakeholder interchange . The REHCCAT initiative works closely with the Federal Ministry of Environment , the National Council on Climate Change , civil society organisations , media institutions , development partners , and the private sector . This collaborative structure gives the project wider legitimacy and increases its chances of sustainability beyond political cycles , while aligning it with Nigeria 's Nationally Determined Contributions and Energy Transition Plan . <p> All rights reserved . nio Guterres , UN secretary-general , said the adoption sends a strong message that addressing climate change is a legal duty , not merely a political choice . <p> The resolution calls on all UN member states to take all necessary steps to prevent significant environmental harm , including emissions generated within their territories , and to fully implement existing commitments under the Paris Agreement . <p> The agreement also urges governments to strengthen cooperation and ensure that climate policies safeguard fundamental rights , including the rights to life , health and adequate living standards . <p> He noted that renewable energy has become the cheapest and most secure energy source globally , adding that keeping global warming within the 1.5 ? C threshold above pre-industrial levels remains achievable . <p> On the vote breakdown , 141 countries supported the resolution , eight opposed it , while 28 abstained . @@137445685 <h> OECD : Developed countries off track on climate adaptation finance <p> A new report by the Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development ( OECD ) has shown that developed countries are not on course to meet the 2025 target of doubling adaptation finance for vulnerable countries . <p> Adaptation finance is money provided to help countries adjust to the effects of climate change that are already happening or expected to happen . <p> At COP26 in Glasgow in 2021 , countries agreed that developed nations should collectively double adaptation finance for developing countries from 2019 levels of $18.8 billion to $40 billion annually by 2025 . <p> However , progress has fallen far short of needs . The United Nations Environment Programme ( UNEP ) , in its Adaptation Gap Report , estimates that developing countries could require between $310 billion and $365 billion annually by 2035 for climate adaptation -- far above the current $40 billion annual target to double adaptation finance from 2019 levels . <p> Advertisement <p> UNEP warned that adaptation needs are now 12 to 14 times higher than current funding flows . It added @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ double adaptation finance by 2025 would likely not be achieved . <p> Ant ? nio Guterres , United Nations secretary-general , had appealed to developed countries to honour their $40 billion adaptation finance pledge to developing nations before the end of 2025 . <p> However , in the report released on Thursday , OECD said adaptation finance reached $34.7 billion in 2024 , up slightly from $33.6 billion in 2023 . Developed countries would still need to provide an additional $5.8 billion in 2025 to meet the target of doubling adaptation finance from 2019 levels . <p> Advertisement <p> For countries like Nigeria , adaptation finance is increasingly critical . The nation has faced recurring floods that have displaced communities and damaged farmlands , desert encroachment in the north , coastal erosion and sea level rise along low-lying areas such as Lagos , and changing rainfall patterns affecting agriculture . <p> African countries contribute relatively little to global emissions but remain among the most exposed to climate impacts . <p> The funding concerns have also emerged amid uncertainty around global climate commitments following the United States ' decision to withdraw @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ <p> The report said climate finance provided and mobilised for developing countries rose from $132.8 billion in 2023 to $136.7 billion in 2024 , surpassing the long-standing target first agreed under UN climate negotiations . The goal was originally meant to be achieved by 2020 , but was only met in 2022 . <p> The report found that most of the funding continued to go towards mitigation projects . <p> It said public finance still dominated overall climate support , while private climate finance mobilised increased sharply to $30.5 billion in 2024 from $22.9 billion a year earlier . <p> Advertisement <p> Attention is now shifting to the New Collective Quantified Goal ( NCQG ) , which replaces the old $100 billion target from 2026 . <p> Under the new framework agreed at COP29 in Azerbaijan , countries aim to scale climate finance for developing nations to at least $1.3 trillion annually by 2035 , with developed countries expected to take the lead in mobilising at least $300 billion annually . <p> Advertisement <p> Much of climate finance has historically come in the form of loans , meaning a significant @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> This has remained a major point of contention at UN climate negotiations , where developing nations have repeatedly argued that climate support , particularly adaptation finance , should come as grants or , at minimum , highly concessional loans with favourable repayment terms .
